Suggested correction: Align the unit title with its content. Either change the title to reflect a general design process unit (e.g., 'Introduction to Design Evaluation') or rewrite the micro-skills to specifically address the evaluation of electrical circuits as suggested by the 'sv-cyt' source.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • major: The primary source for the unit's content, 'sv-cyt', is a science curriculum focused on electricity and magnetism. It does not contain content related to the proposed design skills of defining criteria, evaluating against them, or considering repairability and reuse. Suggested correction: Acknowledge that the unit's content is derived almost entirely from the 'eng-dt' source. The 'sv-cyt' source does not support the pedagogical content of this design unit. The unit should be re-scoped based on evidence that actually supports design process education.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • major: The micro-skill 'propose-repair-reuse' is justified with the quote 'consider the views of others to improve their work'. This evidence does not support the concepts of repair, reuse, or lifecycle extension. Suggested correction: Find a more appropriate evidentiary basis for teaching repair and reuse, or remove the skill if no supporting evidence is available in the provided sources. The connection made is an unsupported inference.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • minor: The micro-skill 'propose-repair-reuse' is presented without prerequisites, but it requires an understanding of how an artifact functions and what its design goals are. Evaluating a broken item for repair is a form of evaluation. Suggested correction: Set 'evaluate-against-criteria' as a prerequisite for 'propose-repair-reuse'. Students should first learn to evaluate a functional or non-functional item against its intended purpose before proposing repairs or alternative uses.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • minor: The assessment for 'propose-repair-reuse' asks students to identify components for repair or reuse from a 'discarded electronic component like a non-working flashlight'. This contradicts the unit's general, non-electrical focus and introduces potential safety and complexity issues not addressed in the prerequisites. Suggested correction: Revise the assessment task to use non-electrical artifacts (e.g., broken mechanical toys, damaged furniture, worn-out textiles) to maintain consistency with the other micro-skills and avoid introducing un-taught electrical concepts.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Internal prerequisite IDs: None proposed

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Sources: source-eng-dt-bac6168fed01542b (page 3, Key stage 2, paragraph-p31-9e287d9b54a4b40e, original characters 202–257)

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Safe circuit measurement

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Distinguish reading a representation from operating a multimeter; no mains electricity, wet circuits or unsupervised battery work.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Measure voltage in a low-voltage DC circuit using a multimeter.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      micro-measure-voltage-multimeter-7905d163e7804c86 · technical_systems · T2 · 60 instruction/practice minutes

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Why this learning matters: This skill is fundamental for understanding electrical circuits. Direct measurement provides concrete experience with abstract concepts like voltage, reinforcing theoretical knowledge. It also introduces students to practical tools and safe handling procedures for electrical measurements.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Proposed grade placement rationale: Sixth graders are developmentally ready for precise measurement tasks and can follow multi-step instructions for tool usage. The curriculum explicitly lists 'Uso del multímetro para medir voltaje' for this grade, indicating its appropriateness.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Evidence of mastery: Students correctly connect a multimeter to measure voltage across components in a simple DC circuit and record the reading with appropriate units.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Assessment task: Given a simple circuit with a battery and one component (e.g., a bulb or a resistor), students will use a multimeter to measure the voltage across the battery and the voltage across the component. They must record their measurements with correct units.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Access and participation: Provide multimeters with large, clear displays. Offer visual aids and step-by-step diagrams for multimeter setup and connection. Pair students with varying abilities to support peer learning. Use color-coded wires for easier identification of positive/negative terminals.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      T0 alternative and limits: Without multimeters, students can only qualitatively observe circuit behavior (e.g., bulb brightness) but cannot quantify voltage, significantly limiting the understanding of electrical principles and practical measurement skills.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Evidence limitations: The source indicates the skill but does not specify pedagogical methods, safety protocols, or the specific types of circuits or components to be used. It also doesn't detail the level of precision expected for measurements.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Safety: All circuit activities must use low-voltage DC power sources (e.g., batteries up to 9V). Students must be supervised at all times. Emphasize never connecting components directly to wall outlets. Instruct students to always disconnect power before making changes to a circuit or measuring resistance. Multimeters should be handled with care and set to the correct function before connecting to a circuit.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Teacher preparation level (0–3): 3

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Success criteria
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student correctly sets the multimeter to measure DC voltage.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student connects the multimeter in parallel across the component being measured.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student reads the multimeter display accurately.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student records the voltage value with the correct unit 'V'.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Teaching sequence
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Diagnosis: Ask students what voltage is and how they think it's measured. Discuss safety precautions for handling electrical components.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Instruction: Explain voltage as electrical potential difference. Demonstrate how to set up a multimeter for DC voltage measurement and connect it in parallel across a component. Emphasize polarity.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Practice: Students work in small groups to measure voltage across different components (e.g., battery, bulb, resistor) in pre-built simple circuits.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Feedback: Circulate to provide immediate feedback on multimeter setup, connection, and reading. Address common errors like incorrect mode selection or series connection.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Misconceptions
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Connecting the multimeter in series to measure voltage.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Incorrectly setting the multimeter to measure current instead of voltage.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Ignoring polarity when connecting the multimeter.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Required resources
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Low-voltage DC power source (e.g., 1.5V or 3V battery packs)
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Multimeters (at least one per two students)
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Resistors (various values)
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Small light bulbs or LEDs with holders
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Connecting wires with alligator clips
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Breadboards or circuit boards
                                                                                                                                                                                                      External knowledge to verify
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Basic concepts of electricity (current, voltage, resistance)
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Safety procedures for low-voltage circuits
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Units of measurement (Volts)
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Unit limitations
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The provided sources do not offer specific pedagogical strategies or detailed lesson plans for teaching these skills, requiring the curriculum designer to infer and create them.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The 'sv-cyt' source is a curriculum document, not an empirical study, so its effectiveness or student outcomes are not validated.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The 'eng-dt' source is from a different educational system (UK Key Stage 2) and requires careful adaptation to the Salvadoran context and 6th-grade level, as its age-appropriateness may differ.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The sources do not provide guidance on how to address diverse learning needs, such as students with disabilities or varying prior knowledge.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The estimated minutes are pedagogical hypotheses and do not account for administrative time, unexpected delays, or individual student pacing differences.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The unit's learning goal 'Represent a low-voltage circuit and measure voltage and resistance' does not fully align with the 'sv-cyt' source, which also explicitly includes 'Mide... corriente eléctrica empleando un multímetro' (Measure... electric current using a multimeter). This unit deliberately scopes out current measurement to focus on foundational voltage and resistance concepts, which may require a revision of the overall learning goal if current measurement is deemed essential for this grade.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Publication corrections after model review
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • A learner can measure voltage on a physically identified circuit without first producing a schematic; notation is a useful teaching route, not a necessary prerequisite.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Uncalibrated model critique: points to review
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • major: The unit's learning goal and its constituent skills deliberately omit the measurement of electric current, despite the primary source curriculum explicitly requiring it. The source 'sv-cyt p.93' states 'Mide voltaje y corriente eléctrica empleando un multímetro' (Measures voltage and electric current using a multimeter). Suggested correction: Either revise the unit's learning goal to include the measurement of electric current, creating a corresponding skill, or provide a stronger pedagogical justification for its exclusion at this grade level, acknowledging the deviation from the source curriculum.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • major: The skill 'measure-voltage-multimeter' has 'represent-series-parallel-circuits' as a prerequisite. This sequence is pedagogically unsound. Students should first learn to build and measure a physical circuit to understand its properties before learning to represent it abstractly with schematic symbols. Suggested correction: Remove the prerequisite link. The pedagogical sequence should be: 1. Build and measure simple circuits (e.g., voltage). 2. Learn to represent the circuits they have built and measured.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • major: The skill 'measure-resistance-multimeter' has 'identify-resistance-color-code' as a prerequisite. This is not a necessary dependency and creates a rigid learning path. A student can learn to measure resistance with a multimeter without first knowing the color code system. Suggested correction: Remove the prerequisite. These two skills are complementary methods for determining resistance and can be taught in either order or concurrently.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • minor: The assessment task for 'measure-voltage-multimeter' requires measuring voltage across a battery and a component. However, the success criteria do not explicitly require measuring voltage across two different points, only that the student can perform the measurement action. Suggested correction: Refine the success criteria to align with the assessment task. For example, add a criterion like: 'Student successfully measures and records voltage across both the power source and a load component in a simple circuit.'
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • minor: The skill 'represent-series-parallel-circuits' cites 'eng-dt p.3 ¶paragraph-p32-c26bfd917edd278f' as evidence. This source mentions 'series circuits' but does not mention 'parallel circuits' or the act of 'representation' using schematic diagrams. Suggested correction: Remove the 'eng-dt' citation for this skill as it does not fully support the claim. The 'sv-cyt' source is sufficient.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • minor: The assessment task for 'identify-resistance-color-code' requires students to determine the 'nominal resistance value and tolerance'. However, the success criteria only mention calculating the nominal value and stating the tolerance, but not combining them into a final statement (e.g., '1000Ω ±5%'). Suggested correction: Add a success criterion that requires the student to state the final resistance value including its tolerance, to fully match the assessment task.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Internal prerequisite IDs: None proposed

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Sources: source-sv-cyt-e86fe5c5cf1a4905 (page 93, Grade 6, unit 3, paragraph-p98-31ad23c9515bf5d6, original characters 294–354); source-sv-cyt-e86fe5c5cf1a4905 (page 94, Grade 6, unit 3, paragraph-p99-1377e731cd53eecd, original characters 779–847)

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Measure resistance of a component using a multimeter.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      micro-measure-resistance-multimeter-d7458973855bae82 · technical_systems · T2 · 50 instruction/practice minutes

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Why this learning matters: Measuring resistance directly with a multimeter provides a practical understanding of this fundamental electrical property. It complements the theoretical concept of resistance and allows students to verify resistor values, which is crucial for circuit design and troubleshooting.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Proposed grade placement rationale: The curriculum explicitly mentions 'Identificación del valor de resistencias eléctricas según... multímetro' for 6th grade, confirming its relevance. This skill builds on prior knowledge of basic circuit components.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Evidence of mastery: Students correctly set up and use a multimeter to measure the resistance of various resistors and record the readings with appropriate units.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Assessment task: Students will be given a set of five unmarked resistors. For each resistor, they must use a multimeter to measure its resistance and record the value in ohms.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Access and participation: Provide multimeters with auto-ranging capabilities to simplify range selection. Use large, easy-to-handle resistors. Offer tactile feedback for multimeter dial settings. Provide a checklist for measurement steps.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      T0 alternative and limits: Without multimeters, students cannot quantitatively determine resistance. They would rely solely on resistor color codes (if taught) or qualitative observations of circuit behavior, which is less precise and limits practical application.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Evidence limitations: The source mentions identifying resistance by multimeter but does not detail the specific procedure, safety considerations for isolating components, or the expected accuracy of measurements. It also doesn't specify the range of resistance values appropriate for this grade.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Safety: All circuit activities must use low-voltage DC power sources (e.g., batteries up to 9V). Students must be supervised at all times. Emphasize never connecting components directly to wall outlets. Instruct students to always disconnect power before making changes to a circuit or measuring resistance. Multimeters should be handled with care and set to the correct function before connecting to a circuit.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Teacher preparation level (0–3): 3

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Success criteria
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student correctly sets the multimeter to measure resistance (ohms).
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student ensures the component is isolated from any power source before measurement.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student connects the multimeter leads correctly across the resistor.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student reads the multimeter display accurately.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student records the resistance value with the correct unit 'Ω'.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Teaching sequence
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Diagnosis: Ask students what resistance is and why it's important in a circuit. Discuss how they might determine a resistor's value.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Instruction: Explain resistance and its unit (ohms). Demonstrate how to set up a multimeter for resistance measurement. Emphasize that resistance must be measured on an unpowered, isolated component.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Practice: Students work in groups to measure the resistance of various resistors. Compare measured values with nominal values (if known).
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Feedback: Provide guidance on multimeter range selection and proper isolation of components. Discuss discrepancies between measured and nominal values due to tolerance.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Misconceptions
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Attempting to measure resistance in a powered circuit.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Incorrectly interpreting the multimeter display for different resistance ranges.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Believing that resistance only applies to specific components, not wires or other parts.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Required resources
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Multimeters (at least one per two students)
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Various resistors (e.g., 100Ω, 1kΩ, 10kΩ)
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Connecting wires
                                                                                                                                                                                                      External knowledge to verify
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Concept of electrical resistance
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Units of measurement (Ohms)
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Basic safety for handling electronic components
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Unit limitations
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The provided sources do not offer specific pedagogical strategies or detailed lesson plans for teaching these skills, requiring the curriculum designer to infer and create them.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The 'sv-cyt' source is a curriculum document, not an empirical study, so its effectiveness or student outcomes are not validated.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The 'eng-dt' source is from a different educational system (UK Key Stage 2) and requires careful adaptation to the Salvadoran context and 6th-grade level, as its age-appropriateness may differ.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The sources do not provide guidance on how to address diverse learning needs, such as students with disabilities or varying prior knowledge.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The estimated minutes are pedagogical hypotheses and do not account for administrative time, unexpected delays, or individual student pacing differences.

This unit deliberately scopes out current measurement to focus on foundational voltage and resistance concepts, which may require a revision of the overall learning goal if current measurement is deemed essential for this grade.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Represent simple series and parallel electrical circuits using standard schematic symbols.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      micro-represent-series-parallel-circuits-0026e7c00977cece · technical_systems · T0 · 45 instruction/practice minutes

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Why this learning matters: Schematic representation is a universal language in electronics. This skill allows students to communicate circuit designs clearly, understand how circuits are built from diagrams, and visualize the flow of electricity without needing physical components. It's a foundational skill for design and analysis.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Proposed grade placement rationale: Sixth graders are capable of abstract representation and following conventions. The curriculum explicitly requires 'Representa circuitos eléctricos en serie y en paralelo', making this a core skill for the grade.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Evidence of mastery: Students draw accurate schematic diagrams for given series and parallel circuit configurations, correctly using standard symbols for components.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Assessment task: Students will be given descriptions or physical examples of two simple circuits (one series, one parallel) and asked to draw their corresponding schematic diagrams using standard symbols for battery, switch, wires, and bulbs/resistors.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Access and participation: Provide pre-printed symbol stencils or stickers for students with fine motor skill challenges. Use large, clear examples of schematic diagrams. Offer digital drawing tools if available. Provide a reference sheet of symbols.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      T0 alternative and limits: Not applicable

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Evidence limitations: The source states the need to represent circuits but does not specify which standard symbols to use, the complexity of circuits to be represented, or the level of detail required in the drawings. It also doesn't mention the pedagogical approach.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Safety: All circuit activities must use low-voltage DC power sources (e.g., batteries up to 9V). Students must be supervised at all times. Emphasize never connecting components directly to wall outlets. Instruct students to always disconnect power before making changes to a circuit or measuring resistance. Multimeters should be handled with care and set to the correct function before connecting to a circuit.

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Teacher preparation level (0–3): 2

                                                                                                                                                                                                      Success criteria
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student correctly identifies and uses standard symbols for all circuit components (battery, switch, resistor/bulb, wire).
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student accurately depicts the series connection of components.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student accurately depicts the parallel connection of components.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Student draws clear, legible diagrams with straight lines for wires.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Teaching sequence
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Diagnosis: Ask students to draw a simple circuit they've seen or built. Discuss the challenges of communicating their design.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Instruction: Introduce standard schematic symbols for common circuit components (battery, switch, bulb/resistor, wire). Explain the conventions for drawing series and parallel connections. Demonstrate drawing simple series and parallel circuits.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Practice: Students practice drawing various simple series and parallel circuits from verbal descriptions or physical models. Provide templates or symbol sheets.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Feedback: Review student drawings, highlighting correct symbol usage and connection types. Correct common errors like inconsistent symbols or ambiguous connections.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Misconceptions
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Drawing components as they appear physically rather than using schematic symbols.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Confusing series and parallel connections in diagrams.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Omitting wires or drawing them as curved lines instead of straight lines.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Required resources
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Paper and pencils
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Rulers
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Reference sheet of standard circuit symbols
                                                                                                                                                                                                      External knowledge to verify
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Basic understanding of series and parallel circuit configurations
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • Common electrical components and their functions
                                                                                                                                                                                                      Unit limitations
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The provided sources do not offer specific pedagogical strategies or detailed lesson plans for teaching these skills, requiring the curriculum designer to infer and create them.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The 'sv-cyt' source is a curriculum document, not an empirical study, so its effectiveness or student outcomes are not validated.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The 'eng-dt' source is from a different educational system (UK Key Stage 2) and requires careful adaptation to the Salvadoran context and 6th-grade level, as its age-appropriateness may differ.
                                                                                                                                                                                                      • The sources do not provide guidance on how to address diverse learning needs, such as students with disabilities or varying prior knowledge.
