                                                                                                                                                                                                        Identify the nominal resistance value of a resistor using its color code.

                                                                                                                                                                                                        micro-identify-resistance-color-code-5958d52707fec435 · technical_systems · T0 · 40 instruction/practice minutes

                                                                                                                                                                                                        Why this learning matters: Understanding resistor color codes is a practical skill for anyone working with electronics. It allows for quick identification of component values without needing a multimeter, fostering independence and efficiency in circuit building and repair. It also reinforces number sense and pattern recognition.

                                                                                                                                                                                                        Proposed grade placement rationale: The curriculum explicitly includes 'Identificación del valor de resistencias eléctricas según código de color' for 6th grade, making it an age-appropriate and required skill. It involves pattern recognition and simple decoding, suitable for this age group.

                                                                                                                                                                                                        Evidence of mastery: Students correctly determine the resistance value and tolerance of various resistors by interpreting their color bands.

                                                                                                                                                                                                        Assessment task: Students will be given five resistors with different color codes. For each resistor, they must write down its nominal resistance value and tolerance based on the color bands.

                                                                                                                                                                                                        Access and participation: Provide large, clear color code charts. Use resistors with distinct, easily discernible color bands. Offer digital tools or apps that help decode color codes (e.g., using a phone's camera to identify colors). For students with color blindness, provide access to a multimeter (from 'measure-resistance-multimeter' skill) as an alternative method to verify the value independently.

                                                                                                                                                                                                        T0 alternative and limits: Not applicable

                                                                                                                                                                                                        Evidence limitations: The source only mentions 'identificación' but does not specify the number of bands (e.g., 4-band, 5-band), the range of resistance values, or the pedagogical approach for teaching the color code. It also doesn't address potential issues like color blindness.

                                                                                                                                                                                                        Safety: All circuit activities must use low-voltage DC power sources (e.g., batteries up to 9V). Students must be supervised at all times. Emphasize never connecting components directly to wall outlets. Instruct students to always disconnect power before making changes to a circuit or measuring resistance. Multimeters should be handled with care and set to the correct function before connecting to a circuit.

                                                                                                                                                                                                        Teacher preparation level (0–3): 2

                                                                                                                                                                                                        Success criteria
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Student correctly identifies the first and second significant digits from the color bands.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Student correctly identifies the multiplier from the third color band.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Student correctly identifies the tolerance from the fourth color band.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Student calculates the nominal resistance value accurately.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Student states the tolerance correctly.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        Teaching sequence
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Diagnosis: Show students a resistor and ask how they might know its value. Discuss the need for a standardized system.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Instruction: Introduce the resistor color code chart (e.g., 'BB ROY G BIV' mnemonic). Explain how to read 4-band resistors (first digit, second digit, multiplier, tolerance). Demonstrate with examples.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Practice: Students work with physical resistors and a color code chart to identify values. Provide worksheets with resistor images for additional practice.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Feedback: Review answers, correcting misinterpretations of color bands or calculation errors. Emphasize the importance of reading bands in the correct order.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        Misconceptions
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Reading the color bands in the wrong order.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Confusing the multiplier band with a significant digit band.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Incorrectly interpreting the tolerance band.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        Required resources
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Various resistors (4-band, different values)
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Resistor color code charts (printed or projected)
                                                                                                                                                                                                        External knowledge to verify
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Basic number recognition and multiplication
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • Understanding of electrical resistance
                                                                                                                                                                                                        Unit limitations
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • The provided sources do not offer specific pedagogical strategies or detailed lesson plans for teaching these skills, requiring the curriculum designer to infer and create them.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • The 'sv-cyt' source is a curriculum document, not an empirical study, so its effectiveness or student outcomes are not validated.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • The 'eng-dt' source is from a different educational system (UK Key Stage 2) and requires careful adaptation to the Salvadoran context and 6th-grade level, as its age-appropriateness may differ.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • The sources do not provide guidance on how to address diverse learning needs, such as students with disabilities or varying prior knowledge.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • The estimated minutes are pedagogical hypotheses and do not account for administrative time, unexpected delays, or individual student pacing differences.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        • The unit's learning goal 'Represent a low-voltage circuit and measure voltage and resistance' does not fully align with the 'sv-cyt' source, which also explicitly includes 'Mide... corriente eléctrica empleando un multímetro' (Measure... electric current using a multimeter). This unit deliberately scopes out current measurement to focus on foundational voltage and resistance concepts, which may require a revision of the overall learning goal if current measurement is deemed essential for this grade.
                                                                                                                                                                                                        Publication corrections after model review
                                                                                                                                                                                                          Uncalibrated model critique: points to review
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • major: The unit's learning goal and its constituent skills deliberately omit the measurement of electric current, despite the primary source curriculum explicitly requiring it. The source 'sv-cyt p.93' states 'Mide voltaje y corriente eléctrica empleando un multímetro' (Measures voltage and electric current using a multimeter). Suggested correction: Either revise the unit's learning goal to include the measurement of electric current, creating a corresponding skill, or provide a stronger pedagogical justification for its exclusion at this grade level, acknowledging the deviation from the source curriculum.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • major: The skill 'measure-voltage-multimeter' has 'represent-series-parallel-circuits' as a prerequisite. This sequence is pedagogically unsound. Students should first learn to build and measure a physical circuit to understand its properties before learning to represent it abstractly with schematic symbols. Suggested correction: Remove the prerequisite link. The pedagogical sequence should be: 1. Build and measure simple circuits (e.g., voltage). 2. Learn to represent the circuits they have built and measured.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • major: The skill 'measure-resistance-multimeter' has 'identify-resistance-color-code' as a prerequisite. This is not a necessary dependency and creates a rigid learning path. A student can learn to measure resistance with a multimeter without first knowing the color code system. Suggested correction: Remove the prerequisite. These two skills are complementary methods for determining resistance and can be taught in either order or concurrently.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • minor: The assessment task for 'measure-voltage-multimeter' requires measuring voltage across a battery and a component. However, the success criteria do not explicitly require measuring voltage across two different points, only that the student can perform the measurement action. Suggested correction: Refine the success criteria to align with the assessment task. For example, add a criterion like: 'Student successfully measures and records voltage across both the power source and a load component in a simple circuit.'
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • minor: The skill 'represent-series-parallel-circuits' cites 'eng-dt p.3 ¶paragraph-p32-c26bfd917edd278f' as evidence. This source mentions 'series circuits' but does not mention 'parallel circuits' or the act of 'representation' using schematic diagrams. Suggested correction: Remove the 'eng-dt' citation for this skill as it does not fully support the claim. The 'sv-cyt' source is sufficient.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • minor: The assessment task for 'identify-resistance-color-code' requires students to determine the 'nominal resistance value and tolerance'. However, the success criteria only mention calculating the nominal value and stating the tolerance, but not combining them into a final statement (e.g., '1000Ω ±5%'). Suggested correction: Add a success criterion that requires the student to state the final resistance value including its tolerance, to fully match the assessment task.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Internal prerequisite IDs: None proposed

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Sources: source-sv-cyt-e86fe5c5cf1a4905 (page 93, Grade 6, unit 3, paragraph-p98-31ad23c9515bf5d6, original characters 199–273)

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Evaluating AI-assisted outputs

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Compare rule-based and learned behavior conceptually; use teacher-provided examples rather than student accounts or personal data.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Identify the source of an AI-assisted output and its potential implications for reliability.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          micro-identify-ai-output-source-148dab02523879f9 · data_and_ai · T1 · 45 instruction/practice minutes

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Why this learning matters: Before evaluating the content of an AI output, students must understand where it comes from. This foundational skill helps them recognize that AI tools are products of specific developers and data, which influences their output. This is a prerequisite for critical evaluation.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Proposed grade placement rationale: In 6th grade, students are ready to move beyond simply consuming digital content to critically examining its origins, especially with emerging AI tools. This aligns with developing digital literacy and critical thinking skills.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Evidence of mastery: Student correctly identifies the source of a given AI-generated text or image and explains one potential implication of that source on its reliability.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Assessment task: Given three texts, one clearly labeled as human-written, one clearly labeled as AI-generated by Tool A, and one clearly labeled as AI-generated by Tool B, students will identify which text is human-written and explain one reason why knowing the source (human vs. AI, or specific AI tool) is important for evaluating its reliability.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Access and participation: Provide visual cues and clear labels for AI tools. Allow students to use text-to-speech readers for instructions and AI outputs. Offer simplified language for explanations.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          T0 alternative and limits: Without a teacher device, students cannot directly observe or interact with diverse AI outputs, limiting their ability to identify sources and understand their implications. The skill would be reduced to theoretical discussion without practical examples.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Evidence limitations: The provided evidence broadly discusses source credibility for 'data, information and their digital content' but does not specifically mention AI-generated content. The application to AI outputs is an extension based on the general principle of source evaluation.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Safety: No specific safety notes are required for this unit as it does not involve physical components or electrical circuits. Ensure responsible internet use and data privacy when accessing AI tools or external information sources.

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Teacher preparation level (0–3): 2

                                                                                                                                                                                                          Success criteria
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Correctly identifies the human-written text.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Provides a relevant reason for source importance (e.g., 'different tools have different purposes', 'some tools are known to be more factual', 'human authors have intentions').
                                                                                                                                                                                                          Teaching sequence
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Diagnosis: Ask students what they know about where information on the internet comes from.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Practice: Show examples of AI outputs from different tools (e.g., ChatGPT, Bard, Midjourney) with clear labels or screenshots of the tool's interface. Discuss their visible characteristics and stated origins. Guide students to identify the tool used or if it's human-generated.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Feedback: Provide immediate feedback on source identification and facilitate a discussion on why different sources might produce different types or qualities of output.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          Misconceptions
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • All AI outputs are equally reliable regardless of the tool or data used.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • AI outputs are always factual because they come from a computer.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          Required resources
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Teacher device with internet access
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Projector
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Examples of AI-generated content from various tools (text, images) with clear source labels
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Examples of human-written content
                                                                                                                                                                                                          External knowledge to verify
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • Basic understanding of what an 'author' or 'creator' is for traditional media.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          Unit limitations
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • The provided evidence offers general guidance on information literacy and evaluation but does not specifically detail pedagogical approaches for AI output evaluation in primary education. This unit is forward-looking and extends existing principles of source evaluation to the new context of AI.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • The unit assumes teacher familiarity with various AI tools and their common outputs/limitations.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • The effectiveness of these micro-skills relies on access to diverse and age-appropriate AI examples, which may vary in availability.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          • The unit focuses on evaluating AI outputs, not on the ethical implications of AI development or advanced AI concepts, which are beyond the scope of this grade level.
                                                                                                                                                                                                          Publication corrections after model review
                                                                                                                                                                                                            Uncalibrated model critique: points to review
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • minor: The provided evidence from DigComp and the Argentine curriculum supports the general evaluation of digital information sources but does not explicitly mention Artificial Intelligence. The proposal's extension of these principles to AI is a reasonable interpretation but is not directly supported by the text of the cited sources. Suggested correction: Acknowledge that this unit is forward-looking and applies established principles of information literacy to the emergent context of AI, as the provided evidence does not specifically address AI-generated content.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • minor: The assessment task for 'identify-ai-output-source' asks students to identify a human-written text from a set of three, and then explain why knowing the source is important. This assesses comparison and explanation, but not the primary skill of identifying the source of a *given* AI output as stated in the 'Evidence of Mastery'. Suggested correction: Align the assessment task with the stated mastery evidence. For example, provide a single output and ask students to identify its source from a list of options (e.g., Human, Tool A, Tool B) based on contextual clues or interface screenshots, and then explain the implication.

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Internal prerequisite IDs: None proposed

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Sources: source-digcomp-d371a960318a1a45 (page 16, Information and data literacy, paragraph-p96-fb94dc1a33cad9c6, original characters 103–159)

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Compare an AI-assisted output with provided factual evidence to identify discrepancies.

                                                                                                                                                                                                            micro-compare-ai-output-to-evidence-dfd1a093d459f63c · data_and_ai · T1 · 60 instruction/practice minutes

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Why this learning matters: This skill is central to evaluating AI outputs. It moves students from passive reception to active verification. By comparing AI-generated content with reliable sources, students learn to critically assess information and understand that AI can make errors or 'hallucinate'.

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Proposed grade placement rationale: Sixth graders are developing stronger analytical skills and can handle comparing multiple pieces of information. This skill builds on earlier information literacy by applying it to a new, complex type of content (AI-generated).

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Evidence of mastery: Student accurately identifies at least two discrepancies between a given AI-generated text and a provided factual source (e.g., a short encyclopedia entry or a verified news article).

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Assessment task: Students receive an AI-generated paragraph about a historical event and a short, factual text (e.g., from a textbook or a reputable online encyclopedia) on the same event. They must highlight or list at least two specific points where the AI output differs from the factual text.

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Access and participation: Use color-coding or graphic organizers to help visually compare texts. Provide audio versions of both the AI output and the factual evidence. Allow extra time for processing and comparison.

                                                                                                                                                                                                            T0 alternative and limits: Without digital access, AI outputs would need to be printed, and factual evidence would also need to be printed. The dynamic nature of AI interaction and the ease of digital comparison would be lost, making the activity more static and less engaging.

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Evidence limitations: The Argentine curriculum mentions 'evaluación y presentación de información' generally. It does not specifically detail the comparison of AI outputs with evidence, but the principle of evaluating information applies directly.

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Safety: No specific safety notes are required for this unit as it does not involve physical components or electrical circuits. Ensure responsible internet use and data privacy when accessing AI tools or external information sources.

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Teacher preparation level (0–3): 2

                                                                                                                                                                                                            Success criteria
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Identifies at least two factual inaccuracies or significant omissions in the AI output compared to the evidence.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Clearly articulates the discrepancy (e.g., 'AI says X, but the text says Y').
                                                                                                                                                                                                            Teaching sequence
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Diagnosis: Present a simple statement and ask students how they would check if it's true.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Practice: Model comparing a short AI-generated text with a reliable source, highlighting differences. Provide pairs of AI output and factual evidence for students to practice identifying discrepancies.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Feedback: Review identified discrepancies as a class, discussing common errors in AI outputs and effective comparison strategies.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            Misconceptions
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • If an AI says something, it must be true.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • All information found online is equally reliable.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            Required resources
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Teacher device with projector
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Printed or digital copies of AI-generated texts and corresponding factual evidence
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Highlighters or digital annotation tools
                                                                                                                                                                                                            External knowledge to verify
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Basic reading comprehension skills.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • Understanding of factual vs. non-factual statements.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            Unit limitations
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • The provided evidence offers general guidance on information literacy and evaluation but does not specifically detail pedagogical approaches for AI output evaluation in primary education. This unit is forward-looking and extends existing principles of source evaluation to the new context of AI.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • The unit assumes teacher familiarity with various AI tools and their common outputs/limitations.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • The effectiveness of these micro-skills relies on access to diverse and age-appropriate AI examples, which may vary in availability.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            • The unit focuses on evaluating AI outputs, not on the ethical implications of AI development or advanced AI concepts, which are beyond the scope of this grade level.
                                                                                                                                                                                                            Publication corrections after model review
                                                                                                                                                                                                              Uncalibrated model critique: points to review
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • minor: The provided evidence from DigComp and the Argentine curriculum supports the general evaluation of digital information sources but does not explicitly mention Artificial Intelligence. The proposal's extension of these principles to AI is a reasonable interpretation but is not directly supported by the text of the cited sources. Suggested correction: Acknowledge that this unit is forward-looking and applies established principles of information literacy to the emergent context of AI, as the provided evidence does not specifically address AI-generated content.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • minor: The prerequisite structure is logical, but the first skill, 'identify-ai-output-source', is not strictly necessary for the second, 'compare-ai-output-to-evidence'. A student can compare a text labeled 'AI-generated' to a factual source without first needing to know how to distinguish between different AI tools. Suggested correction: Consider making 'identify-ai-output-source' a co-requisite or an independent skill. Alternatively, reframe the unit to emphasize that understanding the source is a foundational aspect of digital literacy before proceeding to direct comparison, justifying the current sequence.

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Sources: source-argentina-d3ce0ed43af1a812 (page 20, Primary second cycle, paragraph-p113-695922b327fa7b1d, original characters 1502–1599)

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Explain that AI can make mistakes due to data limitations and that humans are responsible for verifying AI outputs.

                                                                                                                                                                                                              micro-explain-ai-uncertainty-and-responsibility-f746bcd5f77e2889 · data_and_ai · T1 · 55 instruction/practice minutes

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Why this learning matters: This skill addresses the 'why' behind AI errors and the ethical implications. Understanding data limitations helps demystify AI, while emphasizing human responsibility promotes critical thinking and ethical digital citizenship. It moves beyond just identifying errors to understanding their root causes and human agency.

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Proposed grade placement rationale: By 6th grade, students can grasp abstract concepts like 'data limitations' and 'responsibility'. This prepares them for more complex discussions about AI ethics and reliability in later grades.

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Evidence of mastery: Student provides a simple explanation (e.g., 1-2 sentences) for why an AI might produce incorrect information (linking to data) and states that a human must check AI work.

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Assessment task: After reviewing an AI output that contained errors, students will answer: 'Why do you think the AI made mistakes, and whose job is it to make sure the information is correct?'

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Access and participation: Use visual metaphors (e.g., a puzzle with missing pieces for data limitations). Provide sentence starters for explanations. Allow students to express their understanding through drawing or verbal explanation if writing is a barrier.

                                                                                                                                                                                                              T0 alternative and limits: Without a teacher device to show examples of AI outputs and discuss their origins, the explanation of AI uncertainty and data limitations becomes more abstract. The direct link between observed AI errors and their underlying causes would be harder to establish.

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Evidence limitations: The evidence refers to identifying relevance and reliability in general searches. Applying this to explaining AI uncertainty and human responsibility is an interpretation that extends the concept of reliability to the AI generation process itself.

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Safety: No specific safety notes are required for this unit as it does not involve physical components or electrical circuits. Ensure responsible internet use and data privacy when accessing AI tools or external information sources.

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Teacher preparation level (0–3): 2

                                                                                                                                                                                                              Success criteria
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Mentions that AI learns from data and if the data is incomplete or biased, the AI can make mistakes.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Clearly states that humans are responsible for checking and verifying AI-generated information.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              Teaching sequence
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Diagnosis: Ask students if computers always know everything and why or why not.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Practice: Discuss how AI learns from data (analogy: AI is like a student who only reads certain books). Explain that if the 'books' (data) are incomplete or wrong, the AI's 'answers' might be too. Emphasize that humans are the final checkers. Use examples from previous activities.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Feedback: Facilitate a class discussion on student explanations, reinforcing the concepts of data influence and human oversight.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              Misconceptions
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • AI is intelligent like a human and can 'think' for itself.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • AI is always objective and unbiased.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Once AI is created, it doesn't need human supervision.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              Required resources
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Teacher device with projector
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Whiteboard or chart paper for concept mapping
                                                                                                                                                                                                              External knowledge to verify
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Basic understanding of cause and effect.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • Concept of responsibility in daily life.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              Unit limitations
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • The provided evidence offers general guidance on information literacy and evaluation but does not specifically detail pedagogical approaches for AI output evaluation in primary education. This unit is forward-looking and extends existing principles of source evaluation to the new context of AI.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • The unit assumes teacher familiarity with various AI tools and their common outputs/limitations.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • The effectiveness of these micro-skills relies on access to diverse and age-appropriate AI examples, which may vary in availability.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              • The unit focuses on evaluating AI outputs, not on the ethical implications of AI development or advanced AI concepts, which are beyond the scope of this grade level.
                                                                                                                                                                                                              Publication corrections after model review
                                                                                                                                                                                                                Uncalibrated model critique: points to review
                                                                                                                                                                                                                • minor: The provided evidence from DigComp and the Argentine curriculum supports the general evaluation of digital information sources but does not explicitly mention Artificial Intelligence. The proposal's extension of these principles to AI is a reasonable interpretation but is not directly supported by the text of the cited sources. Suggested correction: Acknowledge that this unit is forward-looking and applies established principles of information literacy to the emergent context of AI, as the provided evidence does not specifically address AI-generated content.
                                                                                                                                                                                                                • minor: The assessment task for 'explain-ai-uncertainty-and-responsibility' asks two questions: 'Why do you think the AI made mistakes, and whose job is it to make sure the information is correct?'. This is an open-ended prompt that assesses student opinion ('Why do you think...') rather than their ability to apply the taught concepts of data limitations and human responsibility. Suggested correction: Rephrase the assessment task to directly measure the learning objective. For example: 'Based on what we learned, give one reason an AI might make a mistake and state who is responsible for checking the information before using it.'
